  [0] PhD in Electronic and Computer Engineering | DEPARTMENT OF ELECTRONIC & COMPUTER ENGINEERING
  [19] DEPARTMENT OF ELECTRONIC & COMPUTER ENGINEERING
  [28] BEng in Microelectronics and Integrated Circuits (MEIC)
  [40] Academic Advising (ELEC2910/3910)

  [89] DEPARTMENT OF ELECTRONIC & COMPUTER ENGINEERING

  [158] DEPARTMENT OF ELECTRONIC & COMPUTER ENGINEERING
  [163] The Doctor of Philosophy (PhD) program caters for students who wish to pursue a career in advanced industrial research and development, or university research and teaching. It emphasizes training in original thinking and independent research.
  [165] All postgraduate students must adhere to the curriculum of the year of entry throughout their program of study. Details of the degree requirements can be found in the Postgraduate Program & Course Catalog of the year of entry.
  [168] PhD students are not allowed to use any undergraduate courses for postgraduate credit. The 15 credits must be earned from postgraduate courses, of which at least 9 in the area of Electronic and Computer Engineering (ELEC).
  [170] In addition to the program requirements specified above, students could opt for the Nanotechnology Concentration, Energy Technology Concentration and/or Scientific Computation Concentration. Details can be found in Postgraduate Program & Course Catalog of the year of entry.
  [173] The minimum grade-point average required to maintain satisfactory academic standing in the ECE Department is 2.850. The minimum grade required for each PG course is C. Courses failing to meet this requirement will not be counted toward the candidate's program and the student may be required to retake those courses. According to University regulations, a student cannot take a course more than twice.
  [175] Full-time research student is required to be on campus full-time and consequently in such geographical proximity as to be able to participate fully in University activities associated with the program. Residency provides the student with an opportunity to become immersed in the intellectual environment of the University. The Residency Requirements could be found in Handbook for Research Postgraduate Studies.
  [177] Each student should identify a permanent academic advisor (thesis supervisor) not later than the first semester after he/she is admitted into the program. The advisor will monitor the progress of the candidate, guide the candidate during research, and advise the candidate on academic matters.
  [178] PhD research is conducted under the general supervision of a thesis supervision committee of at least two members, one of whom is the thesis supervisor. When the thesis is ready for examination, an examination committee consisting of the chairman and five faculty members is formed, one of whom is the thesis supervisor. The thesis examination committee has the authority to recommend the conferment of the degree after the student has successfully passed the final thesis defense examination.
  [180] PhD students (effective from Fall Term, 2025/26 intake cohort) are required to pass a qualifying examination within 18 months (for full-time) or 36 months (for part-time) after the commencement of their PhD program, with a maximum of two attempts. The qualifying examination consists of a written proposal and an oral presentation. Students should select a topic among the eight basic areas on which the ECE Department focuses on - Biomedical Engineering, Control and Robotic Systems, Data Science and AI, Integrated Circuits and Systems, Microelectronics, Photonics, Quantum Engineering, Wireless Communications and Networking. The examination format may be subject to revision by the Department as deemed necessary. The student will acquire the PhD Candidate status after passing the PhD qualifying examination.
  [182] The Final Thesis Defense is administered by Thesis Examination Committee (TEC) consisting of six members: the thesis supervisor, two academic staff members within the department, one academic staff member from outside the department, and one examiner external to the HKUST who has expertise in the field being examined. The committee is chaired by an individual from outside the student’s major department, who is appointed by the Dean.

  [36] MSc in Entrepreneurial Leadership in Biotechnology (ELBT)
  [40] The MPhil program provides basic research training in major areas of life science. Students will acquire the knowledge, skills, and experience required for research. A candidate for an MPhil degree is expected to demonstrate knowledge in the discipline and to discover new findings contributing to the field.
  [41] The program normally takes two years of full-time study to complete. Students are expected to conduct thesis research, undertake coursework, attend and present research seminars. Submission and successful defense of a thesis based on original research is required.
  [43] The PhD program aims to cultivate qualified students who can independently conduct advanced-level research projects. Students will gain both broad knowledge in modern life science and in-depth knowledge in specialized areas. A PhD candidate must pass a qualifying examination set by LIFS and is expected to contribute original knowledge to the field of life science by publishing their findings in peer-reviewed international journals.
  [44] The program usually takes four to five years of full-time study to complete. Students are expected to conduct thesis research, undertake coursework, attend and present research seminars. Submission and successful defense of a thesis based on original research is required.
  [46] Students with Bachelor's or MSc degrees who are aiming for PhD are encouraged to enroll in the MPhil program (2 years) first. After writing up and successfully defending an MPhil thesis as well as passing a qualifying exam, the student can be re-admitted into the PhD program (3 years) and will obtain both MPhil and PhD degrees in five years (i.e., 2+3 mode).
  [47] Alternatively, a student may still opt for a straight 4-year PhD program with the support of his/her thesis supervisor. Approval will be granted to exceptional cases such as awardees of the HK PhD Fellowships.
  [54] Molecular and Cellular Neuroscience
  [59] Students with a Bachelor’s and/or Master’s degree in the biological sciences, chemical sciences, physical sciences may apply for postgraduate degree programs in LIFS.
  [60] A minimum average grade of B or above is expected for all courses taken by an applicant.
  [61] Transcripts from an applicant’s previously-attended institution(s) and two letters of recommendation from academic referees are required.
  [63] Applicants whose first language is not English and whose degree or equivalent qualification was awarded by an institution where the medium of the instruction was not English are required to fulfill the minimum English language requirements of the University (i.e., TOEFL iBT: 80; IELTS: 6.5).
  [64] For details please refer to Admission Requirements.
  [66] At least 11 credits for MPhil or 18 credits for PhD from the life science postgraduate course list for Cohort 2022/23 and before. At least 10 credits for MPhil or 17 credits for PhD from the life science postgraduate course list for Cohort 2023/24 and thereafter.
  [72] For details please refer to MPhil/PhD Programs in Life Science.

  [86] MPhil and PhD in Chemical and Biomolecular Engineering
  [98] MSc in Biomolecular Engineering and Health Informatics
  [115] Funding
  [116] https://fytgs.hkust.edu.hk/funding
  [124] Applicants seeking admission to a research degree program should satisfy the following minimum entrance requirements and demonstrate their English ability to undertake the research
  [125] Master of Philosophy (MPhil)
  [126] hold a relevant Bachelor's degree with first or second class honours, or equivalent qualification, in a relevant Science or Engineering disciplines from a recognized university; or
  [127] hold a taught Master's degree or equivalent qualification, in a relevant Science or Engineering discipline from a recognized university.
  [129] hold a relevant Bachelor's degree with first or second class upper honours, or equivalent qualification, in a relevant Science or Engineering discipline from a recognized university; or
  [130] hold a Master's degree, or equivalent qualification, in a relevant Science or Engineering discipline from a recognized university which includes a research thesis.
  [131] Fulfill the English Language requirements with one of the following proficiency attainments:
  [142] If your first language is not English, and your bachelor’s degree or equivalent qualification was not awarded by an institution where the medium of instruction was English, you will need to fulfill these English Language requirements.
  [144] Applicants should submit their applications through the Online Admissions System. Non-local applicants are encouraged to submit their applications as early as possible in order to allow sufficient time to obtain a visa for studying in Hong Kong. More details of the application can be found in the "Admission Requirements" and "Application" tabs of Bioengineering program page.
  [145] For further details about postgraduate admissions, please visit the "Fok Ying Tung Graduate School" website and ask your question via FAQ online.
  [147] For BIEN MPhil & PhD Programs, please send an email to BIEN Program Office.

  [20] The Master of Philosophy (MPhil) Program aims to train students to conduct independent research in Atmospheric Environmental Science. A candidate for an MPhil degree is expected to demonstrate knowledge in the discipline and to synthesize and create new knowledge, making a contribution to the field.
  [21] The Doctor of Philosophy (PhD) Program seeks to train students in original research in Atmospheric Environmental Science and to cultivate independent and innovative thinking that is essential for a successful research career in environmental science. A candidate for a PhD degree is expected to demonstrate mastery of knowledge in the chosen discipline and to synthesize and create new knowledge, making an original and substantial contribution to the discipline.
  [22] Currently, the research concentrations include:
  [26] Environmental sensing and measurement technology

  [124] Applicants seeking admission to a research degree program should satisfy the following minimum entrance requirements and demonstrate their English ability to undertake the research
  [125] Master of Philosophy (MPhil)
  [126] Obtained a bachelor's degree with at least second upper class honors, GPA 3.0 or above from a recognized institution, or an approved equivalent qualification, in a relevant Science or Engineering; or
  [127] Obtained a taught master’s degree, or equivalent qualification, in a relevant Science or Engineering discipline from a recognized institution.
  [129] Obtained a master's degree, or equivalent qualification, in a relevant Science or Engineering discipline from a recognized institution;
  [130] In exceptional circumstances, obtained a bachelor’s degree with a proven record of outstanding performance, for example a first class honors, GPA 3.2 or above, degree from a recognized institution, may be admitted to the program without a master's degree. These students may be required to take additional credits to fulfill the program requirements.
  [131] Fulfill the English Language requirements with one of the following proficiency attainments:
  [144] If your first language is not English, and your bachelor’s degree or equivalent qualification was not awarded by an institution where the medium of instruction was English, you will need to fulfill these English Language requirements.
  [146] Applicants should submit their applications through the Online Admissions System before Deadline. Applications fee should be pay by VISA or MasterCard. For further details about postgraduate admissions, please visit the Office of Postgraduate Studies website.
  [148] You should scan and upload documents which will help program offices consider your application, e.g. bachelor’s/master’s transcripts and certificates, TOEFL/IELTS score reports, etc., to the Online Admissions System before submitting your application.
  [149] Don't worry if you have not yet obtained the required test results e.g. IELTS, TOEFL, GMAT or GRE in the application stage. Provide in the online application a date by which such results are expected to be available. When the scores become available, fill them in through the Online Admissions System and upload the score reports.
  [150] Remember that you will need to send official documents to substantiate your qualifications/ exam results after accepting an admission offer.
  [151] All documents submitted to the University will be used to substantiate your qualifications in confirming the admission offer. Any misrepresentation or forged items found will lead to the disqualification of your application and the withdrawal of your offer. Under the Crimes Ordinance (Cap 200) in Hong Kong, forgery is an offense and the offender is liable on conviction on indictment to imprisonment.
  [153] You are required to nominate 2-5 referees to support your application. Upon your application submission, the referees will be invited by email to complete a reference report online. Referees may attach additional reference letters to the online report if they wish to.
  [155] Face/Phone/Skype Interviews are a required part of the admissions process. Interview details will be sent to shortlisted candidates.
  [156] Offer Acceptance and Program Registration Procedures
  [157] Applicants can log on to the Online Admissions System to check the status of their applications. In addition, applicants should look out for e-mail notifications sent from the University. Upon receipt of an offer, applicants should follow the steps advised by the office of Postgraduate Studies to accept the offer.

=== https://cbe.hkust.edu.hk/academic/postgraduate/cbme-overview | FINAL: https://cbe.hkust.edu.hk/academic/postgraduate/cbme-overview | STATUS: 200 | METHOD: http | LEN: 2748
  [0] MPhil and PhD in Chemical and Biomolecular Engineering - Introduction | Department of Chemical and Biological Engineering
  [86] MPhil and PhD in Chemical and Biomolecular Engineering
  [98] MSc in Biomolecular Engineering and Health Informatics
  [103] MPhil and PhD in Chemical and Biomolecular Engineering
  [104] The Master of Philosophy (MPhil) Program aims to strengthen students’ fundamental knowledge of Chemical Engineering, with specialization in the areas of chemical processing, materials, environment, energy and bioengineering. Students will be exposed to relevant issues involved in the scientific research, technology development and commercial applications in the field. A candidate for an MPhil degree is expected to demonstrate knowledge in the discipline and to synthesize and create new knowledge, making a contribution to the field.
  [105] The Doctor of Philosophy (PhD) Program aims to instill in students the skills needed to identify research issues in Chemical Engineering, formulate an original research plan, and develop creative and innovative solutions that address the research issue. A candidate for a PhD degree is expected to demonstrate mastery of knowledge in the chosen discipline and to synthesize and create new knowledge, making an original and substantial contribution to the discipline.

=== https://ieda.hkust.edu.hk/eng/detail.php?catid=3&sid=65&tid=81 | FINAL: https://ieda.hkust.edu.hk/eng/detail.php?catid=3&sid=65&tid=81 | STATUS: 200 | METHOD: http | LEN: 5018
  [0] HKUST IEDA - PhD in IEDA | Research Postgraduate Programs | ACADEMICS |
  [24] Advisory Board
  [28] BEng in Decision Analytics
  [29] BEng in Industrial Engineering and Engineering Management
  [30] Dual Degree Program
  [74] Students who wish to pursue a career in advanced industrial research and development, or university research and teaching can apply to join the Department's PhD program. This program emphasizes training in original thinking and independent research. Students can design their program of study to suit their interests and needs. The program should cover a specialized area in industrial engineering and at least one minor area, and must be approved by the thesis advisory committee.
  [75] The PhD program seeks to develop a student's skills in identifying issues related to a theoretical problem or a practical application, formulating an original research project that addresses some of the significant issues, and independently creating an effective solution to the problem. Supervised by a Department faculty member, a PhD candidate is expected to demonstrate mastery of knowledge in the chosen discipline and to synthesize and create new knowledge, making an original and substantial contribution to the discipline.
  [77] (Applicable to student cohort admitted from Fall 2021/22)
  [78] Students are required to complete a minimum of 24 credits of approved coursework, including the following IEDA required courses. Students entering with master's degrees from other universities may be granted credit transfer of up to 12 credits.
  [79] IEDA 5230 - Deterministic Models in Operations Research
  [80] IEDA 5250 - Stochastic Models in Operations Research
  [81] IEDA 5270 - Engineering Statistics and Data Analytics
  [83] take one 3-credit course at Engineering School (non-IEDA) 5000 level or above
  [84] take and pass PDEV 6800 Introduction to Teaching and Learning in Higher Education
  [85] take and pass PDEV 6770 Professional Development for Research Postgraduate Students
  [86] take and pass ENGG 6780 Career Development for Engineering Research Students
  [87] pass the one-credit LANG 5001 Postgraduate English for Engineering Research Studies course, though credit earned from LANG 5001 cannot be counted toward degree requirements. Students can be exempted from taking LANG 5001 with the approval of the Department Head and Postgraduate Coordinator, based on students’ background and/or the HKUST Language Center’s English proficiency assessment
  [88] take and pass IEDA 6800 Departmental Seminar for at least four semesters during their residency. Part-time students may be exempted from this requirement
  [89] pass the qualifying examination, pass the preliminary oral examination to review and approve the student’s written research proposal, write a thesis and pass a final thesis examination
  [90] Further details on the program requirement and courses offered can be found at the PG Program Catalog and PG Course Catalog respectively.

=== https://cse.hkust.edu.hk/pg/admissions/ | FINAL: https://cse.hkust.edu.hk/pg/admissions/ | STATUS: 200 | METHOD: http | LEN: 3274
  [50] Higher Education World University Rankings, CSE department is ranked 1st
  [51] in Hong Kong in the Subject of Computer Science. Our department has
  [52] produced pioneering work in several areas, including but not limited to,
  [53] artificial intelligence, cybersecurity, data and knowledge management,
  [54] human-computer interaction, networking and computer systems, software
  [55] engineering and programming languages, theoretical computer science, and
  [57] Our department consists of over 50 faculty members, about 80 MPhil
  [58] students and 430 PhD students. The MPhil study takes two years, and
  [59] the PhD study four to five years. Many of our postgraduate alumni work at
  [60] major IT companies such as Baidu, Facebook, Google, Huawei, IBM,
  [61] Microsoft, and Tencent, and a significant number of our PhD graduates
  [62] pursue their careers at research institutes and universities all over the
  [63] world. If interested, please check out the departmental web page on where do our PhD graduates go.
  [64] Postgraduate students have sufficient scholarship support during their
  [65] study at HKUST. Each student will receive a generous scholarship of
  [66] approximately HKD229,620 (USD29,200) per year. The annual tuition for the
  [67] 2026/27 academic year is HKD47,000 (USD6,000), which is subject to annual
  [68] adjustments. Local research postgraduate students may be eligible for the
  [69] Tuition Waiver Scheme introduced by the HKSAR Government.
  [70] We recruit postgraduate students in two phases. The first phase, early recruiting, runs from
  [71] late April to mid-August and targets excellent MPhil and PhD applicants.
  [72] After the early recruiting phase, applicants can still send regular
  [73] application packages to the HKUST Postgraduate
  [75] If you have any questions, feel free to contact

  [63] Admissions for Research Postgraduate Programs
  [73] Applicants for postgraduate research programs should normally have a bachelor's degree in industrial engineering or a related field and a proven record of performance. Application details can be found on the website of Office of Postgraduate Studies.
  [74] Students admitted to a postgraduate program must demonstrate competence in the following core areas: (a) linear algebra; and (b) probability and applied statistics. Those deemed inadequate in these areas may be required to take a set of remedial courses prescribed by the Department's postgraduate committee and obtain at least a grade B in these courses.
  [75] For enquiries on our program, please email pgieda@ust.hk. For other admission-related enquiries, please contact Office of Postgraduate Studies.

  [45] The NSNT Program aims at training students with solid knowledge in nano material process, nano material characterization, nano device design and fabrication, nano structural observation and manipulation, etc. The graduates from this program should be creative and able to lead a nano technological project investigation.

  [167] Neuroscience
  [181] School of Science - The Hong Kong University of Science and Technology
  [186] Our research postgraduate programs develop students into scientists capable of conducting independent and original research.
  [187] The programs provide students with advanced knowledge and comprehensive training in research on specific science areas. Our outstanding scholars come from around the world with diversified research expertise.
  [189] Our School offers research postgraduate programs in a wide range of disciplines, leading to Master of Philosophy and Doctoral degrees.
  [192] Our research postgraduate programs aim to provide students with advanced knowledge and comprehensive training in research on specific science areas. The programs focus on developing student’s research competency and equipping students with state-of-the-art expertise in conducting independent and original research.
  [195] The Master of Philosophy and the Doctor of Philosophy degrees involve the completion of coursework in addition to the preparation of research theses. The coursework and thesis components provide students with both generalist knowledge of their subject discipline and mastery of their specialized research areas.
  [198] Interested students should take note of the admission requirements for research postgraduate program applications to the School of Science.
  [201] Only full-time full fee paying registered HKUST research postgraduate students are eligible for student accommodation offered by the University.

  [48] Postgraduate Student Early Recruiting (Fall 2027 Intake)
  [49] The Department of Computer Science and Engineering
  [51] and Technology (HKUST) is recruiting MPhil and PhD students to start
  [52] in Fall 2027. We are looking for outstanding undergraduate students as
  [53] well as graduate students with a Master's degree in Computer Science and
  [56] Higher Education World University Rankings, CSE department is ranked 1st
  [57] in Hong Kong in the Subject of Computer Science. Our department has
  [58] produced pioneering work in several areas, including but not limited to,
  [59] artificial intelligence, cybersecurity, data and knowledge management,
  [60] human-computer interaction, networking and computer systems, software
  [61] engineering and programming languages, theoretical computer science, and
  [63] Our department consists of over 50 faculty members, about 80 MPhil
  [64] students and 430 PhD students. The MPhil study takes two years, and
  [65] the PhD study four to five years. Many of our postgraduate alumni work at
  [66] major IT companies such as Baidu, Facebook, Google, Huawei, IBM,
  [67] Microsoft, and Tencent, and a significant number of our PhD graduates
  [68] pursue their careers at research institutes and universities all over the
  [69] world. If interested, please check out the departmental web page on where do our PhD graduates go.
  [70] Postgraduate students have sufficient scholarship support during their
  [71] study at HKUST. Each student will receive a generous scholarship of
  [72] approximately HKD229,620 (USD29,200) per year. The annual tuition for the
  [73] 2026/27 academic year is HKD47,000 (USD6,000), which is subject to annual
  [74] adjustments. Local research postgraduate students may be eligible for the
  [75] Tuition Waiver Scheme introduced by the HKSAR Government.
  [76] Furthermore, the Hong Kong government selects incoming PhD students to
  [77] award the prestigious Hong Kong PhD Fellowship (HKPF) each year. The amount
  [78] of the HKPF is around HKD28,400 per month, plus a travel allowance of
  [79] HKD14,200 each year. In addition, HKUST gives each awardee a one-time reward
  [80] of HKD40,000. In recent years, our department has been highly successful in
  [81] this scheme, receiving 15 to 19 awards annually.
  [82] Our postgraduate admissions require applicants to have good academic
  [83] records in their areas of study and to have obtained a Bachelor's degree
  [85] Applicants also need to satisfy the University's
  [87] We recruit postgraduate students in two phases. The first phase is
  [88] early recruiting, targeted at excellent MPhil and PhD applicants through
  [90] To apply, you can fill out the preliminary application form
  [91] (registration for early admission has closed) at the departmental
  [93] We will process these online applications and shortlist candidates to
  [94] invite for online or in-person interviews. This year, the early
  [95] admission interviews will start in September 2026. Applicants will be
  [96] notified their interview results one to two weeks after their
  [98] After the early recruiting phase, applicants can still send regular
  [99] application packages to the HKUST Postgraduate
  [101] Finally, we encourage exceptional PhD applicants (top 10% in class,
  [102] average grade higher than 85/100, excellent English) to contact us to
  [103] inquire about the HKPF applications. Please find relevant information at
  [106] If you have any questions, feel free to contact

  [43] HSEO Research Safety Management System (RSMS)
  [77] HSEO Research Safety Management System (RSMS)
  [89] The purpose of the PhD program in Chemistry is to prepare students through the execution and completion of a substantial research project to become mature, independent scientists who themselves are then capable of the design, initiation and execution of their own original projects in academic or industrial environments. The admission into the program is to be regarded as a privilege, and the subsequent execution of the research project by a PhD candidate is a major undertaking. It requires the application of considerable depth and breadth of scholarship, and there must be substantial discovery of new science. The final PhD thesis must reflect clearly and completely the fulfillment of these criteria.
  [90] In fulfilling the degree requirements, the candidate is expected to conduct the thesis research under the supervision of a designated supervisor. In addition, the candidate must attend and present seminars, and undertake course work.

  [88] The postgraduate programs of the Department emphasize training in original research, focusing on fundamental, interdisciplinary and applied areas. Among such areas with greatest potential to achieve significant results of relevance to the growth of technological industry are studies of biologically active molecules, new synthetic methods and materials, and surface chemistry. The Department has modern well equipped laboratories with state-of-the-art instrumentation, glassware, chemicals and other items required for all research activities as covered by the above areas. Research postgraduate students also have access to instrumentation shared with other Departments within the University. Opportunity is provided for research students to participate in both local and overseas conferences.
  [91] For MPhil/PhD degree programs, applicants should:
  [92] have obtained a bachelor's degree from HKUST or a recognized institution, or obtained an approved equivalent qualification; and
  [93] satisfy the school and department as to their fitness to pursue the postgraduate program.
  [94] NON-local students must allow time to obtain a Hong Kong student visa.

=== https://cse.hkust.edu.hk/pg/admissions/faq/ | FINAL: https://cse.hkust.edu.hk/pg/admissions/faq/ | STATUS: 200 | METHOD: http | LEN: 7916
  [0] FAQ for Prospective Postgraduate Students | HKUST CSE
  [51] I did not get the offer in the early recruiting phase, could I still
  [53] How good do I need to be to be accepted as a MPhil
  [57] Will I receive a studentship from the
  [60] I am not from Hong Kong. Can I still apply for a
  [61] PG program in your department? Can I apply for a studentship from your
  [63] Do I need to take the English proficiency test/exam?
  [64] After submitting an application, should I contact individual faculty
  [66] How can I find the research interest of the faculty members?
  [71] Procedure in the HKUST Fok Ying Tung Graduate School (FYTGS) Website.
  [75] Applications will be processed on a continuous basis as they arrive
  [76] until positions are filled. You are thus recommended to apply as soon as
  [78] Q3. I did not get the offer in the early recruiting phase, could I still
  [80] Yes, you can still apply in the regular phase because the 2 phases are
  [81] independent, and we reserve a number of quotas for the regular phase.
  [82] Q4. How good do I need to be to be accepted as a MPhil student?
  [83] There are no hard-and-fast rules for admission to the MPhil program. We
  [84] rank applicants based on our judgment of their background and general
  [85] ability to do a research thesis. In practice this usually means that the two
  [86] most important criteria are your CGA and your letters of recommendation.
  [87] Good letters of recommendation are essential. A letter from a faculty
  [88] member that states he/she is willing to be your research supervisor is even
  [89] better (but uncommon); one that states that she/he is willing to supervise
  [90] you AND fund your studentship directly out of his/her research grants is
  [91] even better (but even rarer). We want to emphasize that the CGA is not
  [92] everything. An applicant with a very strong letter of recommendation might
  [93] be admitted over one with a slightly higher CGA but weaker letters.
  [94] In the past most students with B+/A- CGA or above
  [95] (the exact CGA varied from year to year) and good letters were accepted.
  [96] The exact number of students accepted depends upon many factors including
  [97] number of studentships available to the department and a forecast of the
  [98] number of students expected to graduate that year.
  [99] Q5. Whom should I ask to write my two letters of recommendation?
  [100] The letters of recommendation are used to judge your ability and fitness
  [101] to be a MPhil student so you should ask someone who knows you well enough to
  [102] write something substantial about you, including your creativity,
  [104] Your FYP advisor is a good choice. Another possibility is the instructor
  [105] of any course that you did very well in (a useful `trick' is to find a
  [106] course that you really enjoy and are doing well in and make sure that the
  [107] instructor knows who you are as a person, not just as a name and number on
  [108] assignments. One way of doing this is by asking good questions in class.
  [109] Another is to go to the instructor after class and ask for extra reading
  [110] material and then discuss it with him/her. Who knows; you might even find an
  [111] advisor this way.
  [112] Finally, if you have worked outside UST, especially in an IT related
  [113] job, you can ask your supervisor for a letter.
  [114] Q6. Will I receive a studentship from the department to support my
  [116] The current university guideline is that all full-time research PG
  [117] students should be funded 12 months a year. The studentship support can
  [118] come from the supervisor, the department, the school, and/or the
  [119] university, and can be in the form of Fellowship, Teaching Assistantship
  [120] or Research Assistantship. The support may be dependent on satisfactory
  [121] progress in the PG study or satisfactory work.
  [123] We normally prefer to accept applicants who are applying for full time

  [86] MPhil and PhD in Chemical and Biomolecular Engineering
  [98] MSc in Biomolecular Engineering and Health Informatics
  [105] Research Postgraduates in BIEN involve the completion of coursework and independent research, and successful defense of thesis. The normative periods of study are as follows:
  [106] Master of Philosophy (MPhil)
  [110] 4 years with a first degree only
  [111] 3 years if a relevant research master’s degree was earned prior to entering the PhD program
  [120] Core (3 credit): BIEN5010 or BIEN5040 or BIEN5060 or BIEN5070 or BIEN 5820 or CENG5240
  [122] Restrictive bioengineering electives (3 credit)
  [123] Non-restrictive electives to be determined by supervisor based on relatedness to the research project (6 credits]
  [124] Biological course requirement for non-life science background student
  [125] Other program requirements: LANG5005, PDEV6770, PDEV6800, ENGG6780, BIEN6800, 1 presentation at seminar
  [126] Addition to the existing program requirement: Scientific Computation Concentration (SCC)
  [128] Doctor of Philosophy program in Bioengineering (15 credits)
  [129] Core (6 credit): BIEN5010 or BIEN5040 or BIEN5060 or BIEN5070 or BIEN 5820 or CENG5240
  [131] Restrictive bioengineering electives (3 credit)
  [132] Non-restrictive electives to be determined by supervisor based on relatedness to the research project (6 credits] in which a 3-credit course at 5000-level or above outside the PhD (BIEN) program to meet school requirement
  [133] PhD Qualifying Examination (pass within the first 1.5 years of study)
  [134] Biological course requirement for non-life science background student
  [135] Other program requirements: LANG5005, PDEV6770, PDEV6800, ENGG6780, BIEN6800, 2 presentations at seminar
  [136] Addition to the existing program requirement: Scientific Computation Concentration (SCC)
  [139] When students have completed the degree requirements for an award, or are registered in courses that will complete the requirements for an award, they will be included in the list of potential graduates.
  [140] Students must complete the coursework requirement specified for the MPhil/PhD program during your degree study and attain a pass in the thesis examination.
  [141] At graduation, a graduation grade average (GGA) will be calculated from the courses that are presented for an academic award. The GGA for graduation for all postgraduate programs must at least be 2.850.

=== https://physics.hkust.edu.hk/programs/postgraduate-programs/overview | FINAL: https://physics.hkust.edu.hk/programs/postgraduate-programs/overview | STATUS: 200 | METHOD: http | LEN: 7481

  [52] The Center for the Development of the Gifted and Talented

  [107] The Center for the Development of the Gifted and Talented
  [109] Mini-lectures in science for secondary schools
  [132] The Physics Department offers the following research-based postgraduate programs: MPhil in Physics, PhD in Physics, MPhil and PhD in Nano Science and Technology; and two taught postgraduate programs: MSc in Data-Driven Modeling and MSc in Physics.
  [135] Master of Philosophy and Doctor of Philosophy Programs in Physics
  [136] Master of Philosophy and Doctor of Philosophy Programs in Nano Science and Technology
  [138] The MPhil in Physics program aims at providing students with broad training that emphasizes the development of critical thinking and problem-solving skills.
  [139] The PhD in Physics program aims at providing research-oriented education for students interested in pursuing their long-term careers in basic and/or applied physics. It prepares students for worldwide competition and broad horizons in their career choices.
  [140] The MPhil in Nano Science and Technology program aims at training postgraduate students to conduct independent research in nano science and technology.
  [141] The PhD in Nano Science and Technology program aims at training postgraduate students in carrying out original research in nano science and technology, and to cultivate independent and innovative thinking.
  [142] Scientific computation serves as a bridge between the traditional domains of theoretical and experimental science. The Scientific Computation Concentration offers specialized programs to support the academic development of students in this multi-disciplinary area. Students who undertake discipline-specific research involving scientific computation are strongly encouraged to enroll in this Concentration.
  [143] Our postgraduate programs are backed up by a strong team of faculty members, who specialize in a variety of research interests. The research programs at the Physics Department cover not only traditional physics disciplines, such as condensed matter physics and high energy physics, but also biological physics, mesoscopic physics, computational physics, quantum physics, photonics, soft matter and nanomaterials science and technology. Read more: Research Area.
  [144] Joining the Department of Physics as a postgraduate student is an exciting prospect. The Department has received world recongnization for its research breakthroughs. The strong research culture within the Department, and the university as a whole, has encouraged such success, with frequent collaborations between experimental and theoretical faculty members.
  [145] Our postgraduate programs seek to provide students with a solid grounding in broad physics principles and techniques, an ambience for creative and innovative activities, and opportunities for cross-and inter-disciplinary research. Currently, the Department has over 170 research postgraduate students. In addition, programs of regular visiting faculty members/scholars, overseas exchanges, research awards and fellowships have provided tremendous opportunities to our PG students.
  [148] Master of Science (MSc) Program in Data-Driven Modeling
  [149] Master of Science (MSc) Program in Physics
  [151] The Master of Science (MSc) Program in Data-Driven Modeling is jointly offered by the Department of Physics and the Department of Mathematics. The program aims at training students with some science or engineering background who would like to prepare themselves for careers that require modeling skills based on information extracted from data. This program aims at training graduates to have strong skills in problem solving and logical thinking. These skills are essential for them to become competent in the data science sector. They will be trained to have hands-on experience in analyzing large amount of data, extract significant features from them, and hence provide valuable insights to understand complex situations and facilitate smart decision making for businesses, industries and services.
  [152] The Master of Science (MSc) Program in Physics is offered by the Department of Physics. The program focuses on scientific computing, and advanced materials physics and technology. We aim to upgrade and strengthen students’ knowledge in core areas of physics; and with various elective courses students can choose to specialize in a particular physics area. Students will gain knowledge of advanced materials for applications in next generation devices, and/or hands-on experience of learning various simulation and programming tools to solve physics and real-world problems. The overall aim is to enhance one’s fundamental knowledge in physics, promote critical thinking, problem solving and presentation skills. These important transferable skills are much ought-after by industry and commerce. The program may also be used as a stepping-stone towards a research postgraduate degree.​

=== https://physics.hkust.edu.hk/programs/postgraduate-programs/scholarship-award | FINAL: https://physics.hkust.edu.hk/programs/postgraduate-programs/scholarship-award | STATUS: 200 | METHOD: http | LEN: 5654
  [0] Scholarships & Awards | The Hong Kong University of Science and Technology
  [52] The Center for the Development of the Gifted and Talented
  [54] Mini-lectures in science for secondary schools
  [107] The Center for the Development of the Gifted and Talented
  [109] Mini-lectures in science for secondary schools
  [132] Postgraduate Studentships
  [133] Postgraduate studentship (PGS) is awarded to qualified full-time PhD or MPhil student of the University to provide them with financial assistance during the course of their study. The amount of PGS is HK$229,620 (~US$29,420) per year (rate is for 2026/27 academic year). Read More.
  [134] Other scholarships and awards are based on academic merit, English language proficiency, and the suitability of the research PG students for the proposed program of study, as is the selection of applicants for admission to postgraduate studies. In addition to research works, recipients of studentships are required to engage in teaching assistant (TA) duties as assigned by the Department of Physics, which is part of the program graduation requirements.
  [136] To attract top quality students to pursue PhD studies in Hong Kong, the Government of Hong Kong Special Administrative Region (HKSAR) offers Hong Kong PhD Fellowship Scheme (HKPFS). The scheme is publicly funded and administrated by the Hong Kong Research Grants Council (RGC). Awardee of this Fellowship will receive stipend for 4 years, at a rate of HK$28,700 (~US$3,677) per month and travel allowance of HK$14,400 (~US$1,850) per year, and may have a chance to receive HK$40,000 (~US$5,130) of "Redbird PhD Award" with a tuition fee waiver in the first four years of their PhD studies starting from the 2025/26 academic year. Read More.
  [138] The University offers Research Travel Grant (RTG) program which intends to subsidy the cost of travel for postdoctoral fellows, research graduate students, and undergraduate students who present research papers at academic conferences and professional meetings around the world.
  [139] Full-time research graduate students who have been registered at HKUST for at least one year at the date of the conference/meeting are eligible to apply. The research work being presented at the conference/meetings must have been conducted at HKUST. Read More.
  [141] The Fok Ying Tong Graduate School (FYTGS) of the University will select a number of PhD students every year to receive an award for overseas research attachment program. The Global Research Award for PhD Students aims to enrich students’ research experience by providing them with exposure to foreign cultures and environments, and widening their circle of professional contacts, so as to better prepare students for their future academic careers. Read More.
  [142] RedBird PhD Award / Academic Excellence Awards
  [143] To recognize the outstanding research achievement of research postgraduate students, the School of Science offers prestigious RedBird PhD Award Program. Read More.
  [144] Recruitment Award for new international PhD students: HK$40,000 (~US$5,130) and a waiver of tuition fee in the first year.
  [145] Academic Excellence Award for current PhD students: HK$20,000 (~US$2,560) per year, subject to academic performance.

=== https://physics.hkust.edu.hk/admissions/postgraduate-pg-admissions | FINAL: https://physics.hkust.edu.hk/admissions/postgraduate-pg-admissions | STATUS: 200 | METHOD: http | LEN: 5712

  [43] The Center for the Development of the Gifted and Talented
  [45] Mini-lectures in science for secondary schools
  [89] The Center for the Development of the Gifted and Talented
  [91] Mini-lectures in science for secondary schools
  [105] The Physics Department offers the following research-based postgraduate programs: MPhil in Physics, PhD in Physics, and MPhil and PhD in Nano Science and Technology; and two taught-based postgraduate programs: MSc in Physics and MSc in Data-Driven Modeling.
  [106] The MPhil in Physics program aims at providing students with broad training that emphasizes the development of critical thinking and problem-solving skills. The PhD in Physics program aims at providing research-oriented education for students interested in pursuing their long-term careers in basic and/or applied physics. It prepares students for worldwide competition and broad horizons in their career choices.
  [107] Postgraduate scholarships are provided to qualified full-time research postgraduate students. The awards of the scholarships are based on academic merit and the suitability of the applicant for the proposed program of study, as is the selection of applicants for admission to postgraduate studies. Recipients of studentships are required to engage in ancillary teaching and research duties in the Department.
  [108] Our research-based postgraduate programs are backed up by a strong team of faculty members, who specialize in a variety of research interests. The research programs at the Physics Department cover not only traditional physics disciplines, such as condensed matter physics and high energy physics, but also biological physics, mesoscopic physics, computational physics, quantum physics, photonics, cold atoms, soft matters and nanomaterials science and technology. Read more in the research section.
  [109] The MSc programs aim at training students who would like to prepare themselves for careers that require strong skills in problem solving and logical thinking backed by specific knowledge in data modelling or core areas in physics.
  [110] Joining the Department of Physics as a postgraduate student is an exciting prospect. The Department has received world recognition for its research breakthroughs. The strong research culture within the Department, and the university as a whole, has encouraged such success, with frequent collaborations between experimental and theoretical faculty members.
  [111] The Department has over 190 research postgraduate students. Overseas research exchange awards and Hong Kong PhD Fellowships have provided tremendous opportunities to our postgraduate students. To recognize the outstanding research achievement of research postgraduate students (RPg), the School of Science and the University offer Research Awards for research-based postgraduate students.
  [113] Applicants for postgraduate programs in the Department of Physics are expected to meet the general admission requirements for graduate programs and should possess a good honors degree in physics or related fields. Applicants whose first language is not English and whose degree or equivalent qualification was awarded by an institution where the medium of instruction was not English will also have to meet the English language requirements.
  [114] Selection for admission will be based on academic records and available results of standardized tests in physics, proficiency in the English language, letters of reference and a personal interview at the discretion of the Department.

=== https://physics.hkust.edu.hk/programs/postgraduate-programs/program-nano-science-and-technology | FINAL: https://physics.hkust.edu.hk/programs/postgraduate-programs/program-nano-science-and-technology | STATUS: 200 | METHOD: http | LEN: 4730
  [0] Postgraduate Programs in Nano Science and Technology | The Hong Kong University of Science and Technology
  [52] The Center for the Development of the Gifted and Talented
  [54] Mini-lectures in science for secondary schools

  [114] Postgraduate Programs in Nano Science and Technology
  [132] The Physics Department offers MPhil and PhD Programs in Nano Science and Technology (NSNT). While diversified in its applications, Nano Science and Technology program has three well-defined core elements which define the basis of our teaching/research program. These three elements are: sample fabrication, characterization-experimentation, and theory-simulations.
  [133] The Nano Science and Technology (NSNT) Program aims to educate students with the necessary interdisciplinary knowledge to contribute to the long-term nanoscale research and development, which will lead to potential breakthroughs in areas such as materials and manufacturing, nano electronics, medicine and healthcare, environment, energy, chemicals, and biotechnology. This program allows students to apply the knowledge in physical science to their study in nano science and technology.
  [134] Nano science and technology is perceived as one of the most important area affecting mankind in the 21st century. Nano science and technology is inherently interdisciplinary in nature. By being able to tailor material properties at the most fundamental level, nanotechnology is envisioned to impact virtually everything, from communications to energy production, environmental management, medicine, computing, and education.
  [135] The NSNT Program aims at training students with solid knowledge in nano material process, nano material characterization, nano device design and fabrication, nano structural observation and manipulation, etc. The graduates from this program should be creative and able to lead a nano technological project investigation. Applicants are expected to hold a bachelor’s degree in physics or related field with satisfactory performance.
  [136] For details of the curricula and courses offered by Nano Science and Technology Program, please refer to the following links :

A candidate for an MPhil degree is expected to demonstrate knowledge in the discipline and to synthesize and create new knowledge, making a contribution to the field.
https://prog-crs.hkust.edu.hk/pgprog/2027-28/mphil-phd-mech/ [55]: The Doctor of Philosophy (PhD) Program aims at equipping the students with the capability of identifying and solving academic and/or real problems creatively. A candidate for a PhD degree is expected to demonstrate mastery of knowledge in the chosen discipline and to synthesize and create new knowledge, making an original and substantial contribution to the discipline.
https://prog-crs.hkust.edu.hk/pgprog/2027-28/mphil-phd-mech/ [69]: Micro ElectroMechanical Systems (MEMS) is a multidisciplinary research field which has been making a great impact on our daily life, including various micro sensors used in personal electronics, transportation, communication, and biomedical diagnostics. Fundamental and applied research work is being conducted in this area. Basic micro/ nanomechanics, such as fluid and solid mechanics, heat transfer and materials problems unique to micro/nanomechanical systems are studied. New ideas to produce microsystems for energy, biomedicine and nanomaterials, micro sensors and micro actuators are explored. Technology issues related to the micro/ nanofabrication of these devices are being addressed.

  [39] Students with a Bachelor’s and/or Master’s degree in the biological sciences, chemical sciences, physical sciences may apply for postgraduate degree programs in the Division of Life Science (LIFS). Applicants should have obtained a Bachelor’s degree from a recognized institution with a grade average of 3 or above, or any equivalent qualification. Transcripts from an applicant’s previously-attended institution(s) and two letters of recommendation from academic referees are required.
  [40] Applicants whose first language is not English and whose degree or equivalent qualification was awarded by an institution where the medium of the instruction was not English are required to fulfill the minimum English language requirements of the University (i.e., TOEFL iBT: 80; IELTS: 6.5). For details, please refer to the Admission Requirements.
  [41] Application Deadlines
  [44] Application for admission is completed through our online application system. For fullest consideration, it is in your best interest to complete all parts of the application by or before the deadline. Incomplete applications may not be reviewed. A complete application should include:
  [46] Official Transcripts and Degree Certificates of Academic Qualification
  [52] Additional information about the application process is available on the website of HKUST Fok Ying Tung Graduate School.
  [53] For admission-related enquiries from prospective students and current applicants, please send us a message.

  [0] MPhil and PhD in Chemical and Biomolecular Engineering - Program Details | Department of Chemical and Biological Engineering
  [86] MPhil and PhD in Chemical and Biomolecular Engineering
  [98] MSc in Biomolecular Engineering and Health Informatics
  [103] MPhil and PhD in Chemical and Biomolecular Engineering
  [105] Master of Philosophy (MPhil): Normally 2 years for full-time and 4 years for part-time study.
  [106] Doctor of Philosophy (PhD): Normally 3 years if a relevant master's degree is earned prior to entering the PhD program or 4 years with a first degree for full-time; 8 years for part-time study.
  [114] When students have completed the degree requirements for an award, or are registered in courses that will complete the requirements for an award, they will be included in the list of potential graduates.
  [115] To be considered for graduation, an MPhil or PhD student must:
  [116] Complete the courses specified for the program;
  [117] Achieve a graduation grade average of at least 2.850 in the courses used to satisfy the program requirements;
  [119] Complete the program within the specified time limit; and
  [120] Meet the residency requirements of the program

  [161] All full-time RPg students are required to complete PDEV 6800. The course is composed of a 10-hour training offered by the Center for Education Innovation (CEI), and session(s) of instructional delivery to be assigned by the respective departments. Upon satisfactory completion of the training conducted by CEI, MPhil students are required to give at least one 30-minute session of instructional delivery in front of a group of students for one term. PhD students are required to give at least one such session each in two different terms. The instructional delivery will be formally assessed.

  [0] Postgraduate Programs Admission | DEPARTMENT OF ELECTRONIC & COMPUTER ENGINEERING
  [19] DEPARTMENT OF ELECTRONIC & COMPUTER ENGINEERING
  [28] BEng in Microelectronics and Integrated Circuits (MEIC)
  [40] Academic Advising (ELEC2910/3910)
  [72] ECE Advisory Board
  [89] DEPARTMENT OF ELECTRONIC & COMPUTER ENGINEERING
  [99] BEng in Microelectronics and Integrated Circuits (MEIC)
  [111] Academic Advising (ELEC2910/3910)
  [143] ECE Advisory Board
  [158] DEPARTMENT OF ELECTRONIC & COMPUTER ENGINEERING
  [162] The Department offers postgraduate programs leading to the degrees of Master of Philosophy (MPhil) and Doctor of Philosophy (PhD) in Electronic and Computer Engineering. Full-time and part-time options are available for both MPhil and PhD degrees. The normal study load for full-time MPhil/PhD students is 10 credits per regular term whereas the maximum study load for those in part-time mode is 8 credits per term. We also offer a number of self-financing Master of Science (MSc) programs.
  [164] Applicants for admission to the MPhil or PhD program are required to have completed, by the time they enter HKUST, a Bachelor's degree in Electronic and/or Computer Engineering or a related science or engineering field with high honors. Admission to the postgraduate program will be based on the applicants' academic record and research potential.
  [165] TOEFL or IELTS scores are required for non-local applicants whose primary language is not English and whose degree or equivalent qualification was awarded by an institution where the medium of instruction was not English. Applicants are advised to submit: Originals of qualification documents (e.g. transcripts) and other supporting documents should be submitted directly to HKUST Fok Ying Tung Graduate School (FYTGS) for verification and records.
  [167] Application procedures for the Postgraduate programs can be found on HKUST Fok Ying Tung Graduate School (FYTGS) website.
  [169] Established in 2009 by the Hong Kong Research Grants Council (RGC), the Hong Kong PhD Fellowship Scheme aims at attracting the best and brightest students from around the world to pursue doctoral studies in Hong Kong. Eligible applicants are those seeking admission as new full-time PhD research candidates in Government-funded institutions in Hong Kong irrespective of their country of origin and ethnic background. Applicants should demonstrate outstanding academic performance, and good research ability and potential.
  [171] The Scheme is now open for applications, admitting research postgraduates to conduct cross-disciplinary research under four hubs - Function, Information, Society and Systems.
  [174] Our postgraduates have excellent opportunities to take up senior positions in the industry as electronic engineers, communication engineers, system analysts and designers, information technologists, computer project managers and many other technical and managerial positions.
  [175] Organizations that have employed our postgraduates include world-class local and overseas companies and institutions, such as ASTRI (HK), China Motion Telecom (HK), Ericsson (China), Fujitsu (HK), HKUST (HK), IBM (USA), Intel Corporation (USA), Motorola Semiconductors (HK), Nanyang Technological University (Singapore), National University of Singapore (Singapore), Tsinghua University (China), University of Arizona (USA), University of Manchester (UK), and University of Texas at Dallas (USA).
  [177] For PG admissions, please contact HKUST Fok Ying Tung Graduate School (FYTGS)

=== https://ece.hkust.edu.hk/phdeas | FINAL: https://ece.hkust.edu.hk/phdeas | STATUS: 200 | METHOD: http | LEN: 4747
  [0] PhD Early Admissions Scheme | DEPARTMENT OF ELECTRONIC & COMPUTER ENGINEERING
  [19] DEPARTMENT OF ELECTRONIC & COMPUTER ENGINEERING
  [28] BEng in Microelectronics and Integrated Circuits (MEIC)
  [40] Academic Advising (ELEC2910/3910)
  [72] ECE Advisory Board
  [89] DEPARTMENT OF ELECTRONIC & COMPUTER ENGINEERING

  [143] ECE Advisory Board
  [158] DEPARTMENT OF ELECTRONIC & COMPUTER ENGINEERING
  [163] The PhD Early Admissions Scheme (PhD-EAS) is an elite program created by the ECE department for outstanding students from top universities in China to enter the PhD research early and directly. The PhD-EAS mainly targets Year 3 Undergraduate students. Currently, our partner universities include
  [168] Huazhong University of Science and Technology
  [177] University of Electronic Science and Technology of China
  [178] University of Science and Technology of China
  [182] Over the past years, PhD-EAS students have made outstanding achievements in frontier research. Many PhD-EAS students were awarded Hong Kong PhD Fellowship Scheme (HKPFS). Undergraduates with strong academic profile and research motivation are encouraged to apply for the PhD-EAS through their home universities.
  [184] Home university/ institution to nominate top Undergraduate students
  [186] ECE faculty members to interview candidates in two rounds
  [187] Successful candidates to be informed and asked for acceptance
  [188] PhD-EAS exercise starts in February to March every year. Interested students should ask their home schools about the application. Inquiries can be also sent to eceeas@ust.hk.
  [195] Copyright © The Hong Kong University of Science and Technology. All rights reserved.

=== https://physics.hkust.edu.hk/programs/postgraduate-programs/phd-physics | FINAL: https://physics.hkust.edu.hk/programs/postgraduate-programs/phd-physics | STATUS: 200 | METHOD: http | LEN: 4679
  [0] DOCTOR OF PHILOSOPHY (PHD) IN PHYSICS | The Hong Kong University of Science and Technology
  [52] The Center for the Development of the Gifted and Talented
  [54] Mini-lectures in science for secondary schools
  [107] The Center for the Development of the Gifted and Talented
  [109] Mini-lectures in science for secondary schools
  [132] The PhD degree is conferred primarily in recognition of breadth of scholarship, depth of research, and power to investigate problems independently and efficiently. In fulfilling the degree requirements, the student is expected to undertake the required course work, attend and present seminars, and conduct thesis research.
  [133] During the first two semesters, a full-time PhD student is expected to register in a combined total of 12 credits or more of graduate courses offered by Department of Physics (see physics PG courses offered by the Department). Students are required to complete at least 12 credits of approved Physics postgraduate courses. Full-time students are expected to complete the coursework requirements during the first two regular terms. Students with a first degree in an area other than Physics may be required to take additional courses.
  [134] In addition to physics postgraduate courses, a PhD student is required complete other departmental and university level courses that are part of the degree requirements.
  [135] A student in the PhD program is also required to pass the PhD Qualifying Examination (QE) within specific timelines depending on whether the student has MPhil degree or not. Student with a satisfactory academic records will then be admitted to PhD candidacy after which the student should register in PHYS 7990 Doctoral Thesis Research during regular terms (Fall/Spring term).
  [136] In the final stage of research, a PhD student is required to submit a thesis to the Department and subsequently present and defend it. Any student who has performed unsatisfactorily will be asked to re-submit and/or re-defend the thesis. A student is allowed a total of two attempts at passing the thesis examination. According to the university's guideline, the PhD study period is four(4) years for students who possesses a BSc degree or three(3) years for students who possesses a MPhil degree.

=== https://cbe.hkust.edu.hk/academic/postgraduate/bien-overview | FINAL: https://cbe.hkust.edu.hk/academic/postgraduate/bien-overview | STATUS: 200 | METHOD: http | LEN: 7427
  [0] MPhil and PhD in Bioengineering - Introduction | Department of Chemical and Biological Engineering
  [86] MPhil and PhD in Chemical and Biomolecular Engineering
  [98] MSc in Biomolecular Engineering and Health Informatics
  [104] The Master of Philosophy (MPhil) and Doctor of Philosophy (PhD) Programs in Bioengineering facilitate the close integration of biological, physical, and computational sciences and engineering in the study of biological processes. The programs adopt a systematic and quantitative approach to the study of biological systems. The aim is to provide students with the necessary knowledge to contribute to the biomedical industry as well as to optimize existing bioprocesses, and to develop new biomedical technologies. The Programs are managed by the Department of Chemical and Biological Engineering.
  [105] A candidate for an MPhil degree is expected to demonstrate knowledge in the discipline and to synthesize and create new knowledge, making a contribution to the field.
  [106] A candidate for a PhD degree is expected to demonstrate mastery of knowledge in the chosen discipline and to synthesize and create new knowledge, making an original and substantial contribution to the discipline.
  [107] The BIEN program comprises four concentration streams to address different applications in the bioengineering area. The four streams are:
  [108] Stream I - Biological Information Engineering
  [109] This stream addresses the application of biological information technology to develop new applications and products. Bioinformation engineering can be applied on areas such as protein structure prediction, drug design, biodiversity, data mining and knowledge discovery in molecular databases, data interpretation and evolutionary biology.
  [110] Researchers in this stream aim to create and apply engineering tools to improve the quality of health care and clinical practice. To achieve this goal, statistical/feature-based segmentation methods (e.g. Statistical Decision Theory, Markov Random Fields, EM Algorithm, etc), deformable model based object reconstruction method (e.g. Statistical Decision Theory, Markov Random Field, EM Algorithm, etc), deformable model based object reconstruction methods (e.g. Active Contours/Surfaces, Level Set Methods, etc.), image registration and visualisation methods, biomedical signal modeling processing techniques will be exploited. Potential application will be in the areas of image-guided surgery, such as surgical navigation, surgical planning, and computer-aided diagnostic systems.
  [111] Stream 2 - Bioprocessing and BioProduct Design
  [112] Bioprocessing is the application of chemical engineering principles to the design of biocatalysts, cells, reactors, separation systems and their integration by process systems engineering into an optimal bioprocess plant. In addition to technology aspects, engineers need to be aware of regulatory, safety and ethical issues in biotechnology.
  [113] There is growing interest in bioproduct design in the industries. Researchers are working on the development of high-value-added products such as pharmaceuticals, biopharmaceuticals, natural products, Traditional Chinese Medicines (TCM), personal care and household products. The application of bioproduct design is extensive.
  [114] Stream 3 - BioMEMS / Biosensors / Biomaterials and Biocatalysts
  [115] Biomaterials and biomechanics is the engineering study of the physical and biological properties of synthetic polymer, metal, ceramic and other materials, and their interaction with blood and soft and hard tissues. It aims to provide novel, reliable and safe new materials and devices for biomedical use. Engineers and scientist of different disciplines work closely together for the creation of novel biomaterials and biodevices. Physiologists investigate in novel bone implants whereas chemists create nano-engineered surface coatings for bioanalytics. The research in this stream ranges from the investigation of new engineering devices for rapid detection of food- and air-borne pathogens to the design of engineering materials for artificial organs and bone replacement.
  [116] BioMEMS is the application of biological and bioanalytical methods to micro electrical and mechanical systems such as gene chips.
  [117] Using engineering principles and nano/micro fabrication techniques, we construct miniaturized devices to study biological systems and develop new technologies that enable the realization of point-of-care diagnostic products. Our research is multidisciplinary and involves researchers in various academic departments.
  [118] We are very well positioned at HKUST to perform this research. Our Materials Characterisation and Preparation Facility (MCPF) is equipped with modern technology for in-depth materials study. Microdevices for various applications in life sciences are produced in our Nanoelectronics Fabrication Facility (NFF). The challenge of our work is its multidisciplinary that requires a broad engineering understanding; we are compensated by an emerging field of applications in life sciences and the potential of our work to improve life quality.
  [120] Pharmaceutical engineering emphasizes on the engineering aspects of pharmaceutical production, development, and operations.
  [121] The development of processing technologies for the production of pharmaceuticals is of our major research interest. The advancement in the study on the manufacture of enantiomers and polymorphs, the use of high-dimensional phase diagrams for rapid process synthesis, and the use of systematic methods for process development will definitely enhance the western medicine manufacturing process. For Chinese medicines and dietary supplements, the liquid-liquid extraction, chromatography, crystallization and supecritical fluid technologies are being developed for upgrading the quality of medicines and improving the quality assurance of products. Besides, the design of pharmaceutical dosage forms is also essential in this area of research.
  [123] Copyright © The Hong Kong University of Science and Technology. All rights reserved.
  [130] Copyright © The Hong Kong University of Science and Technology. All rights reserved.

=== https://chem.hkust.edu.hk/programs/PG-overview | FINAL: https://chem.hkust.edu.hk/programs/PG-overview | STATUS: 200 | METHOD: http | LEN: 4917
  [43] HSEO Research Safety Management System (RSMS)
  [77] HSEO Research Safety Management System (RSMS)
  [89] The purpose of the PhD program in Chemistry is to prepare students through the execution and completion of a substantial research project to become mature, independent scientists who themselves are then capable of the design, initiation and execution of their own original projects in academic or industrial environments. The admission into the program is to be regarded as a privilege, and the subsequent execution of the research project by a PhD candidate is a major undertaking. It requires the application of considerable depth and breadth of scholarship, and there must be substantial discovery of new science. The final PhD thesis must reflect clearly and completely the fulfillment of these criteria.
  [90] In fulfilling the degree requirements, the candidate is expected to conduct the thesis research under the supervision of a designated supervisor. In addition, the candidate must attend and present seminars, and undertake course work.
  [91] Master of Philosophy (MPhil) in Chemistry
  [92] The program is designed with flexibility in order that students may tailor course selections according to their needs and interests. Requirements consist of approved course work and an original research thesis at the master’s level. Students with a first degree in an area other than that of their postgraduate program may be required to take additional courses. In fulfilling the degree requirements, students are expected to attend and present seminars, undertake course work and conduct thesis research. In the final stage of the program, students are required to submit a thesis to the Department and, subsequently, to present and defend it.
  [93] Master of Philosophy (MPhil) and Doctor of Philosophy (PhD) In Marine Environmental Science and Atmospheric Environmental Science
  [94] Multidisciplinary programs offered by the School of Science. The Department participates in this program. Further details at envs.ust.hk.
  [96] The MSc program aims to prepare BSc graduates for employment and a career in analytical laboratories. This ranges from materials and forensic testing to environmental sampling and food quality control. The application of modern analysis techniques to real work problems as well as physical principles underlying such methods will be covered. In addition to core and elective lecture modules, the program also includes laboratory and project based practical courses, providing extensive hands-on experience. The program may also be used as a stepping stone to a research postgraduate degree.
  [97] Please refer to The MSc in Analytical Chemistry Program Website
